# Proposed OEIS comments: A229530 to A232118

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A229530

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A229530>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [1172](../papers/01001-01500/01172-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 14*a(n-1) - 57*a(n-2) + 56*a(n-3) - 16*a(n-4) for n>5.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(6 - 48*x + 199*x^2 - 274*x^3 + 105*x^4) / (1 - 7*x + 4*x^2)^2. - _Colin Barker_, Sep 18 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 196 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A229534

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A229534>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [2904](../papers/02501-03000/02904-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k:
> Empirical for row n:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical column recurrences are true. Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A229685

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A229685>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [3614](../papers/03501-04000/03614-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical column recurrences are true. Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A230180>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [9482](../papers/09001-09500/09482-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 26*a(n-1) - 97*a(n-2) + 89*a(n-3) - 18*a(n-4) + a(n-5).
> Empirical g.f.: 3*x*(3 - 25*x + 50*x^2 - 69*x^3 + 9*x^4) / ((1 - x)*(1 - 25*x + 72*x^2 - 17*x^3 + x^4)). - _Colin Barker_, Sep 21 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 6 561 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 6. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A230691

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A230691>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [4176](../papers/04001-04500/04176-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 94 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 152 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 94, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A230802

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A230802>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10857](../papers/10501-11000/10857-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 68 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 71 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 68, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A230942

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A230942>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [9714](../papers/09501-10000/09714-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) + 14*a(n-3) + a(n-4) - a(n-5).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(3 - x + 14*x^2 + x^3 - x^4) / (1 - 3*x - 14*x^3 - x^4 + x^5). - _Colin Barker_, Mar 18 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Only the cells with i+j even carry a value, and the diagonal and antidiagonal neighbours of such a cell are again such cells, so the white squares form a board of their own on which the condition looks one line up and one line down. Three consecutive lines therefore settle it for the middle one, and counting a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 9 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A231129

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A231129>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [4197](../papers/04001-04500/04197-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 90 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 191 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 90, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A231213>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [3368](../papers/03001-03500/03368-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) - a(n-2) + a(n-3) - 2*a(n-4).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(4 - 3*x - x^2 - 2*x^3) / ((1 - x)*(1 - 2*x - x^2 - 2*x^3)). - _Colin Barker_, Sep 27 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A231399

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A231399>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [4254](../papers/04001-04500/04254-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 83 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1594 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 83, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A231705

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A231705>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [263](../papers/00001-00500/00263-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 77 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 190 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 77, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A232038

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A232038>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [564](../papers/00501-01000/00564-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrences in the linked file are correct. Column k counts arrays of a fixed width, so it is a walk count in a finite graph and satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence; Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one, its order is the order stated for that column, and so is the order of every tail, so any recurrence of that order is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A232042

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A232042>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3426](../papers/03001-03500/03426-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 4*a(n-1) + 9*a(n-2) -a (n-3) - 6*a(n-4) for n>5.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(4 - x - 16*x^2 - 5*x^3 + 12*x^4) / (1 - 4*x - 9*x^2 + x^3 + 6*x^4). - _Colin Barker_, Oct 02 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 50 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 78 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one.
```
